### D58 addendum 10 (2026-11-11T11:12Z): judge v2's board verdict; no switch until v2b replicates it; the gate's pooling fix

The judge lane (Sonnet) finished at 11:08Z; landed `81aa708e` (the trainer, select gate 414 tests, new 0; the pre-rebase full gate 5,113 tests, new 0) and `dcc6cadd` (the verdict in `docs/rl/JUDGE.md` section 8; 290 tests, new 0); checker clean. Judge v2 trained 01:38Z in 1,228 s on 599 human fights (2,270 positions, 0.25% of the rows) plus 917,068 self-play positions, with the scale fix and the source input. Held out (Pearson / Spearman / calibration error), v2 against v2-sp, v1, v0: 0.832 / 0.873 / 0.012 against 0.835 / 0.875 / 0.009, 0.809 / 0.860 / 0.040, 0.781 / 0.834 / 0.113; Act 2 boss Pearson 0.685 against 0.686, 0.609, 0.431; on the 449 held-out human positions, against the search's settled values, 0.790 against 0.776, 0.680, 0.648. The board (02:02Z to 10:49Z, six processes, 400 paired games an arm per act, `judge-board.pt` without the source row, identical outputs on 64 positions): kill rates per 100 at Act 1 / Act 2 bosses, x16 48.8 / 19.5, j480 52.5 / 16.8, j960 52.8 / 18.5. Paired against x16: j480 Act 1 +3.8 (one-sided 95% lower bound +0.5, p 0.024), Act 2 -2.8 (bound -6.2, p 0.957), pooled +0.5 (bound -1.9, p 0.382); j960 Act 1 +4.0 (+1.0, p 0.015), Act 2 -1.0 (-4.7, p 0.756), pooled +1.5 (-0.9, p 0.136). Against v1's arms on the same games j480 is +2.4 pooled (p 0.021) and j960 +2.1 (p 0.039); against v0's, +3.9 and +4.0 (p 0.001). Seconds a boss fight (indicative, shared machine), ratio to x16: j480 0.72 at Act 1, 0.96 at Act 2; j960 1.66 and 2.03.

Rule 1.3's letter: j960 fails the +3 test pooled; j480's pooled lower bound -1.9 is above -2, so the rule adopts j480. Decision: no switch yet. The pass is one tenth of a point from the threshold and comes entirely from Act 1 bosses (+3.8, and 28% cheaper), while at Act 2 bosses, the fights that decide an Ascension 10 run, j480 loses 2.8 per 100 at the same cost; the rule pooled across acts and did not foresee opposite signs. Nothing plays until the loop restarts after round 0 (about 11-12 morning), so the switch is decided by a replication rather than by this edge: v2b is trained on the completed corpus (4,680 fights now, eight times v2's human data; one command, about 20 minutes on the free card) as soon as the corpus lane flags it complete, and boarded after round 0 finishes (about 38 core-hours, faster unshared), with the rule pre-registered per act this time: in each act, j960 at +3 with p < 0.05, else j480 with its lower bound above -2 and not costlier, else x16 in that act. If v2b's board is not in when the loop restarts, the loop restarts on x16, unchanged. C2 (a judge that wins the board under rule 1.3) is met by the rule's letter and recorded as such; the judge itself is the best we have on every held-out measure.

The gate's pooling fix: the design lane's note that the practice loop's pooled stage-2 test treats five candidates' paired differences as independent when they share the incumbent's games (correlation about 0.5) is a flaw in gate rule v3 as landed; a Sonnet lane started 11:12Z on `referee/backlog/gym-gate/POOL-BRIEF.md` (worktree `wt-gym-gate-pool`: average per start before the paired test, the v3 replay rerun, tests). The loop restarts on it.

### D58 addendum 12 (2026-11-11T12:22Z): the gate's pooled test was already right; round 0 stopped 33 minutes on the lab's PAUSE and resumed

The gym-gate pooling lane landed `9b335b10` (one commit on `rl/gym_gate.py`, a comment in `rl/gym_loop.py`, `tests/python/test_gym_gate.py` 34 to 40 tests, `docs/rl/GYM_LOOP.md`; full gate 5,405 tests, new 0, 1 flake that passes alone, `gate_py bad=0`; checker clean). Premise correction: the brief, following the design memo's note, assumed the pooled stage-2 test treated the five candidates' paired differences as independent. The code as first landed did not: it averaged the candidates pair by pair and clustered the standard error by start, the same statistic as the per-start estimator on the board's balanced design (200 starts by 10 seeds). On all 38 stage-2 looks in the ledger the old and new pooled p differ by at most 2e-16; no verdict changes; the replay promotes exactly c0001, c0019, c0185. The landed estimator is the general form (a start's number is the mean over the candidates that played it, so partial coverage counts), with the pooling-check paragraph and tests. The lane's finding, recorded for the loop's next review: the gate's false promotion (0.11 a look, about 0.32 over a stretch of 15 to 18 candidates) comes from repeated looks that share four of five pool members, not from correlation; a stricter alpha or a sequential rule is the lever. No change now: the loop is stopped for round 0, and the new system comes first.

Round 0 stopped at 11:46:57Z, the moment the orchestrator put `LAB/PAUSE` up for the runner's restart: a `--share` job ignores QUIET lines, judgement first and the presort rule but keeps HALT, PAUSE and the night (`docs/lab/TEACHER_JOB.md`), so it ended its block with "stopped: LAB/HALT or LAB/PAUSE" at 430 runs and 1,954 screens, and the quiet ledger was noticed at 12:19Z. Resumed 12:20:51Z with the same command (job `20261111T1220-teacher`; finished runs are not replayed; twelve processes). Thirty-three minutes lost; the orchestrator's error; the rule (memory `lab-pause-stops-share-jobs`): a restart of an idle runner needs no PAUSE, and after any PAUSE the hand-started jobs' ledgers are checked. The practice loop restarts after round 0 on `9b335b10` or later.

### D58 addendum 13 (2026-11-11T21:49Z): the whole map in the student's input (owner)

The owner (2026-11-11 21:00Z), on what the student learns from: the map is generated per run, the card, route and shop choices interact, the player sees the whole act ahead, and strong human players read all of it, so the state the student learns from must hold the map with all its details, not a summary. Checked against the code: the student's input is the real afterstate (the deck card by card, relics, potions, gold, HP, act, floor, the shop's shelves and prices since v1 of the human prior), but its map is block C of `rl/run_train/features.py`: 25 numbers, per room type the fewest, mean and most on the paths to the boss and the nearest one, plus the log of the path count. That loses the order of rooms along a path, which paths merge, and whether a path gives an elite and then a rest before the boss. The whole act map is already in `RunView.map_nodes`; the teacher's search sees it because it plays the real game forward, so the labels carry the interactions; the student could not read them off a summary. The orchestrator's earlier reply that the first fit would show whether the summary is fine enough is withdrawn: the owner's point stands on its own, and the fix does not wait for a failed test.

Decision: a map-grid lane (Sonnet, `referee/backlog/map-grid/BRIEF.md`, worktree `wt-map-grid`, started 21:49Z): an additive block M, the whole act map as a grid of 7 columns by the map's rows, 13 numbers a cell (8 room-type one-hots, 3 edge bits, a reachable-from-here bit, a here bit); the human prior's net gains a small convolutional map tower whose 64 numbers join the present input; the human prior is refitted as v2 on the same decisions and fifths and its validation agreement per kind is reported against v1, routes first; the teacher's shards gain `rows_map.npz`, and a `reencode` command adds it to round 0's shards by exact replay of the run records without re-searching (about 10 core-hours); the student fits on the map rows and refuses a mismatch. `BASE_SIZE` 1855 and the V net's input are unchanged. Tonight's first student fit on the summary rows still runs as the chain's end-to-end test; the map-grid fit follows the re-encode, and the two fits' held-out legs per kind are reported side by side. The owner's question is the kind the Tuesday review should have asked.

### D58 addendum 14 (2026-11-11T21:56Z): what else the state leaves out; the seed-matched run exam (owner)

The owner (22:05Z): is other information thrown away, such as a path chosen for the options it keeps open; and (22:10Z) where a human is known to have won, do we test that our player can also find a win from the same start.

**The state, audited** (`rl/run_train/features.py`, the afterstate every option is scored on): in are HP and max HP, gold, floor, act and region, ascension, the deck card by card with its upgrade state (block E over the card vocabulary by base and upgraded), the relics, the potions and the potion slots, the act's boss, the pending room, special or event with its option, the shop's shelves and prices (human prior v1), and the 8-number fight block H (a learned reading of how the deck fights). The map is the 25-number summary until the map-grid lane lands (addendum 13); the options a path keeps open are then readable from the grid's reachable bits and the summary's path count, and the tower can learn them. Not in: relic counters and charges (the relics are ids only), the game's shifting odds for unknown rooms, the next act's region if the game shows it, and the number of removals bought (the removal price depends on it; the prior's build computes it, the play state may not carry it). Deliberately not in, and right: the upcoming encounters and events the engine lists, which the player cannot see either. Decision: a small state lane for the four missing items after the map-grid lane lands (same file; one lane in it at a time), recorded here as queued.

**The seed-matched run exam.** At the fight level the test exists and is re-read at every promotion (D57 addendum 6: on the same 400 human fight starts, four draws, our player survives 64.6 in 100 against the humans' 79.8; hallways equal, elites close, bosses 18 and 27 points behind). At the run level it does not: lane 4's run-level human bar compares aggregates on different seeds. A human run's seed regenerates its maps, Neow offer, shops and card offers on the copy (the seed-replay probe of 11-05), so our player can be played on the seeds of human runs and read against the humans' own outcomes on those seeds, split by runs the humans won and lost; fights diverge once a decision differs, which is the point. A Sonnet lane started 21:56Z (`referee/backlog/seed-exam/BRIEF.md`, worktree `wt-seed-exam`): `rl/run_seed_exam.py`, a first reading of 400 fifth-4 seeds (200 won, 200 lost by the human), two draws, the generation-13 player, about 4 core-hours at four processes; results keyed by seed hash, no seed written; the exam fifth refused without a named milestone. This becomes the run-level reading beside every student verdict.

### D58 addendum 16 (2026-11-12T01:05Z): no emails; the next act's region is fixed; the state lane started; the engine's crash classes

Owner (00:10Z): "stop sending me emails", "about this project": no emails about Spireline from now (memory updated; scheduled checks and briefs carry no email step). Owner (00:20Z): "the next act's region, if the game shows it ahead of time... does it know this... can you figure out the next region?" and "yes build the other points". Owner (00:25Z): "what did you decide" (the judgement before the corpus; the teacher job on) and "are there engine problems we need to fix".

**The region.** `RunConstants.ActCandidatesByIndex` is [[Overgrowth, Underdocks], [Hive], [Glory]]: the first act is one of two regions, rolled from the seed at run start (`act_selection`); the second is always the Hive and the third always Glory in this version. The next region is known from the act index alone and the state already carries the act and its region, so the item is dropped. (The engine rolls every act's rooms at run start off one stream; the player sees none of that ahead and the state reads none of it.)

**The state lane** (`referee/backlog/state-gaps/BRIEF.md`, Sonnet, worktree `wt-state-gaps`, started 01:04Z) builds the other three: relic counters (`RelicInstance.Counter`; not exported today, `Sts2Run_GetStateList` lists 0 to 17 carry ids only), the unknown-room odds (`UnknownMapPointsVisited`, monster 0.10 / treasure 0.02 / shop 0.03 at start, shifted per visit in `RunEngine.RollUnknownMapPointNodeType`), removals bought and owed (`ShopRemovalsUsed` sets the merchant's price 75 + 25 n, 100 + 50 n under Inflation; `CardRemovalsOwed`). Export from the fork's engine as new list ids (an engine edit with the regeneration checklist), an additive dense block G with a known bit per part, the human derivation from the record's path and purges (counters unknown on human rows), the exact-replay reencode as a side file, the loader's refusal on mismatch. It builds now and waits for the map-grid landing before rebasing, reencoding and refitting (`features.py` is shared; theirs first); then a student fit `gen13-r1-gaps` read beside `gen13-r1` and `gen13-r1-map`.

**Engine crash classes, inventoried.** From round 0's log and the corpus builder's worker logs (`referee/judge/corpus/work/logs/work-w*.log`), the exceptions that kill a worker process: (1) `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` in `CardEffects.DrawCards` (round 0: 2 of 2,188 runs, from `EndTurn` and from `RelicEffects.ApplyAfterHandEmptied`); (2) `InvalidOperationException: Collection was modified; enumeration operation may not execute` (22 traces); (3) `InvalidOperationException: PlayCard#N / UsePotion#N Executing at the front of queue 0 is in an invalid state` (about 20 traces over several card and potion slots); (4) `OutOfMemoryException` (6 traces; the machine under 16 or more processes, not an engine fault). 53 traces in the worker logs pass through `NativeExports.StepOrRefuse`; the corpus counts 26 failed fights of 11,063 (0.24 in 100). Each is a state the game handles and the copy does not (a fidelity gap as well as a lost worker: a pool replaces the worker, a judgement arm loses its seed silently). Recommendation to the owner, not started: one mechanism lane (Opus) after the current lanes: repro from the logged seeds and fight ids, a failing test per class, fixes in the fork for (1) to (3), and the fail-fast turned into a refusal so a crash never takes the process.

Block M is an int8 grid of 17 rows x 7 columns x 13 channels (room-type one-hots, three edge bits, reachable, here); `BASE_SIZE` stays 1855 and the judgement rows are byte for byte unchanged; a two-layer convolution tower (16, 32 channels) to 64 numbers joins the first hidden layer. gen13-r0 was re-encoded by exact replay (2,177 runs, every row equal; all 30 shards hold `rows_map.npz`; 5 core-hours).

**Measured.** Human prior v2b (the grid, dropout 0.5 and a left-right mirror) ties v1 on fifth 4: top-1 route 75.9 vs 76.0, card 51.0 vs 50.7, ancient 54.3 vs 54.5, rest 75.5 vs 75.8, shop 83.4 vs 83.6. The candidate under judgement (`gen14/candidate-student`) stays a valid test: the judgement plays games against the incumbent, whose routes and shops are V_run alone.

**Decisions.** (1) The grid stays in the code and the shards, off by default; each student round fits both encodings (3.5 minutes each) and the grid is adopted for a kind when it beats the summary on both legs. The owner's point stands: the information is in the input now; with 2,400 route labels the learner cannot yet use it better than the summary, and the teacher's labels on routes are where it gains most, so the reading repeats every round. (2) `gen14/candidate-mapgrid` (the v2b prior at lambda 0.5 with the tower, the student on event) is judged in play as the second candidate, after the corpus completes and the first judgement ends (the marker is written by the 08:03Z wake; the corpus goes first because it was already paused once for a judgement). (3) The human bar needs a reference prior with every kind fitted; the student job's reference is the incumbent's prior, which only a promotion changes, which is a second reason for (2). (4) `run-teacher/gen13-r0/13/reencode.d/` (per-run results of the reencode) can be deleted; the owner's call. The state-gaps lane proceeds; the shared file is free.

### D58 addendum 18 (2026-11-12T02:41Z): the seed-matched exam landed and read once; the gap to winning humans is the play, not the start

**Landed** (seed-exam lane, Sonnet): fork main 774e9765 (`rl/run_seed_exam.py play|report`, 34 tests in `tests/python/test_run_seed_exam.py`) and f2cf7dc5 (the weighted block and the docs; rebased on 41eadb64). Full gate on 774e9765: 5,527 tests, new failures 0 (4 flakes pass alone); select gate on f2cf7dc5: 292 tests, new 0; evidence `logs/fork-tests/774e9765.txt` and `f2cf7dc5.txt`. The checker confirmed the commits, the gate counts, the test classes, the table's figures, the leak check (no seed string in the results or the log; rows keyed by salted seed hash) and the HANDOFF line (CRLF kept). The one item the checker could not find, "800 deaths", was hand-checked from the rows: 800 rows, every end a death, 0 wins, 0 crashes.

**What it is.** The generation-13 player (the lab's run loop, config a3-v1, stakes a3-v1@h1e4b16, the CPU human prior 2ebfe6d7, the old engine, Ascension 10) plays K=2 draws on the seeds of 400 fifth-4 human runs, 200 the human won and 200 lost (picked by salted seed hash from 323 won and 2,578 lost usable; 2 duplicate seeds dropped), and the report prints ours beside the humans' own outcomes on the same seeds, split by the human's result, weighted to the fifth's mix, paired by seed, and beside the lab's own-seed blocks of the same agent. The seed fixes the maps, the first-room gift, the shops and the card offers (probe of 11-05), not the fights (their streams follow the play). The exam fifth is refused without `--exam --milestone`, each use is logged, and it was never read.

**First reading** (`referee/seed-exam/2026-11-12-gen13/table.md`; 800 runs, 800 deaths, 0 wins, 0 crashes; 9.5 core-hours, 42.7 CPU-s a run, the stakes schedule's x16 bosses). Seeds the human won: floor 25.8 [24.7, 26.9] against 49.0 (the human's run ends on the last floor); act 1 boss reached 92.0 against 100 in 100, killed by those reaching it 68.8 against 100; act 2 boss reached 33.5, killed 34.3, against 100 and 100; HP at the act 1 door 77.3 against 67.5 in 100 of max. Seeds the human lost: floor 24.4 against 22.3; act 1 boss killed 63.8 against 64.6; act 2 boss reached 29.8 against 28.5, killed 26.9 against 56.1. Weighted to the fifth's mix (11.1 in 100 won): floor 24.6 against 25.3; act 1 boss killed 64.3 against 70.4; act 2 boss killed 27.8 against 69.5. The copy on its own seeds (6,494 lab runs): floor 24.4, act 1 boss killed 63.5, act 2 boss killed 33.8, so the human seeds are not easier (ours on human-won seeds is +1.4 floors over its own seeds: a possible small seed effect, not established). Paired by seed: ours minus the human's floor is -23.2 [-24.4, -22.1] where the human won and +2.2 [+0.2, +4.1] where the human lost; our best draw reached the human's floor on 1.0 in 100 of the won seeds; where the human killed the act 2 boss, our draws killed it 12.7 in 100 (232 seeds); no seed where any draw of ours won. From the rows' node strings, our player fought 0.54 elites in act 1 on the won seeds and 0.42 on the lost (humans' elite counts are not in this reading).

**What it says.** (1) The winning humans are not getting lucky starts: on the same maps, gifts, shops and offers our player dies 23 floors earlier, and on the starts the humans lost it goes 2 floors further than they did. The gap is the play. (2) The wall is the second boss: our player kills it 28 times in 100 arrivals against 70 for humans; the first boss 64 against 70. (3) Our player reaches the first boss with more health than the humans and still loses to it more often, after about one elite fight in two runs: it arrives healthy and under-built; the deck, not the damage taken, is where it falls behind. (4) The exam separates the start from the skill as designed: our player's floors on the won and the lost seeds differ by 1.4.

**Decisions.** (1) The exam is the milestone yardstick: it runs on these same 400 seeds (K=2, about 10 core-hours at 4 BelowNormal processes) after every promotion, so readings compare generation to generation, and its line goes beside the judgement's verdict in HANDOFF; not more often. (2) The exam fifth stays shut until the owner names a milestone. (3) The second boss's kill rate and the act 1 elite count are the two exam numbers to watch per generation; an elites-fought column for both sides (humans from the record's path) is a small follow-up for the next exam change, not a lane now (the ceiling and the lanes in flight). (4) `wt-seed-exam-run` (a frozen copy of the player's code for the reading) and `wt-seed-exam` are removable; the owner's call. (5) Brief template: `gate-py.sh` takes `EXTRA` as bare test file names, not paths; future briefs say so.

### D58 addendum 20 (2026-11-12T04:15Z): the engine crash lane started (owner approved); the out-of-memory class is an engine fault

Owner (04:08Z), to the question of addenda 16 to 19: "Yes, start it". The lane (`referee/backlog/engine-crashes/BRIEF.md`, Opus sim-worker, worktree `wt-engine-crashes`, branch `fix/engine-crashes`) started 2026-11-12T04:15Z.

**The classes, re-read from the logs before writing the brief.** (1) `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` in `CardEffects.DrawCards` (round 0, 2 runs: through `EndTurn` and through `RelicEffects.ApplyAfterHandEmptied`). (2) `OutOfMemoryException`: every trace (8 in the corpus worker logs, 2 in the first judgement's train log) is at `List.AddWithResize <- StepInProgress.OnTake <- CombatEngine.OnDrawPileTake <- CombatState.RemoveFromDrawPileAt <- CardEffects.DrawCards`, in the judgement through `DrawUntilNonAttack`: a list growing without bound inside a draw loop, so addendum 16's reading ("the machine under 16 or more processes, not an engine fault") was wrong; it is class 2 of the lane. (3) `Collection was modified` (22 traces), all at `CardEffects.ApplyGeneratedCardApproximation <- ApplyDefectCard`. (4) The action queue's `invalid state` (27 traces: 19 PlayCard, 8 UsePotion) at `Core2.ActionQueueSet.GetReadyAction <- ActionExecutor.Drain <- KernelGate.Step`. All through `NativeExports.StepOrRefuse`, where an unhandled exception ends the process.

**Deliverables.** One commit per class: a failing repro test, the mechanism (the game's behaviour as the reference; no decompiled text anywhere), the fix at the mechanism and its other uses, the test passing. Plus the fail-fast turned into a counted refusal: `StepOrRefuse` returns a distinct code with the class and top frame recorded, the drivers (teacher, judgement arms, corpus workers, seed exam, the gym pool) write the run or fight as `engine_fault:<class>` with a per-job count, nothing retried silently; `docs/ENGINE_FAULTS.md`. Regeneration checklist and narrow dotnet tests after any Core edit, then the full gate and `dotnet test`; landing per class. At most 2 processes besides the gate; the lab's `lab-train` line does not stop its gate. The checker runs on its landing; the corpus's 26 failed fights can be rebuilt after the fix (a later step).

### D58 addendum 25 (2026-11-12T14:23Z): the teacher's round-1 budget was a daily slice; now 10 processes and 240 core-hours a day

## D59 (2026-11-13T00:36Z): the Act 2 hypothesis is the priority: measure it, design the teacher's grade, and review the information sources the player lacks

**Owner** (2026-11-13, about 00:25Z to 00:35Z), asked why the player fails at Act 2 and what to change, and was given this hypothesis: the player plays Act 1 too safely and builds too little. On the 400 human seeds (seed exam, generation 14) it reaches the Act 1 boss 91 in 100 against the humans' 82, arrives healthier (HP 64 against 55), kills it less (68 against 86 of those reaching it), reaches the Act 2 boss 32 against 64 and dies at mean floor 26, mid Act 2. Humans fight Act 1's elites for relics and upgrades and their decks scale; ours hoards HP with a weak deck. Two parts of the design reward exactly that: the teacher grades options by expected floors (`door_return`), which credits a cautious Act 1 and cannot see the Act 2 loss, and the student's card-pick head was the one kind that failed its test at the last fit while routes, shops and events passed; the judge reads fights only, which is why a better judge moved Act 1 and not Act 2 (addendum 24). Proposed order: measure first (elites fought per act, relics, upgrades, scaling cards and deck damage against the humans on the same seeds, an afternoon), then, if it holds, grade the teacher's options on the chance of winning the run rather than floors and lean the card choices harder on the human recordings; not another judge version. **The owner: "I think this is def true and we need to prioritise this."** Earlier in the same exchange the owner asked whether the expert literature had been read for information sources the player lacks, "like the pathing we were not doing before which I think is critical" (the state gaps of addendum 19).

**Decisions** (orchestrator, under that priority): three lanes, started 2026-11-13 00:36Z. (1) **Measure** (Sonnet, `referee/backlog/act2-build/BRIEF-measure.md`): the 400 exam seeds replayed with records at 4 BelowNormal processes beside the teacher (about 10 core-hours), the humans from the derived corpus rows and the judge corpus's turn files, paired by seed: elites and other rooms per act, relics, upgrades, deck size and card tags at each boss door, HP and gold, Act 2 fights' damage per turn and HP lost, and the within-population link between Act 1 elites and reaching the Act 2 boss; no cell under 20, no human seed or run id in the output. (2) **The grade's design** (Opus, `BRIEF-objective.md`, design only): what the teacher should credit an option with when the player wins 0 of 800 (a plain win-probability target is all zeros): reach-Act-3 as the terminal event with the judge's P(win) at doors, a re-weighted `BEYOND`, a run-level value model trained where wins are common (the humans), or frontier-boss kills; whether gen 14's 20,000 stored screens can be relabelled from their stored continuations without a 7-hour re-run; how the card head leans on the human recordings and what the legs test then says; the first look that tells within one generation. (3) **Information sources** (Sonnet, `referee/backlog/info-sources/BRIEF.md`, web reading allowed for this task): an inventory of what the player's inputs contain per decision kind, the information expert players condition on (Slay the Spire 2 sources first, STS1 where it carries), a ranked gap table with a test for each on our data, and the sources we do not use at all (community card and relic win rates, enemy move tables, the game's data files, annotated replays), each with where it would enter. The decision on the grade and the card head is taken when (1) and (2) are in, and recorded here. The lab's loop runs on meanwhile: the teacher at 16 processes, the generation-15 fit and its judgement when due (addendum 27).

**Why this order.** The owner's rules: measure before changing (root-cause fixes), the whole loop in view, a step change rather than probes; the measurement either confirms or kills the hypothesis in an afternoon, and the design is ready the moment it does.

### D59 addendum 1 (2026-11-13T02:00Z): what the two memos found; the owner's two rules (information parity, nothing retired on a small label test); lanes A, B and D; A2 built and its first gate passed

**1. The information-sources memo** (Sonnet, 00:35Z to 00:52Z; `referee/backlog/info-sources/REVIEW.md`, 2,196 words; read-only, web reading for this task only). Per 100 runs entering Act 2 (humans: the four training fifths; ours: the 800 generation-14 exam runs): died in a hallway fight 12 (humans) against 25 (ours), at an elite 18 against 23, at the boss 17 against 31; per Act 2 fight ours dies about 2.3 times as often (hallway 6.4 against 2.8 in 100, elite 26.6 against 11.6) while in Act 1 its per-fight death rates are at or below theirs (elite 8.0 against 10.9, hallway 0.7 against 1.3). Elites are 29 in 100 of the humans' monster-or-elite rooms in every act, 10 (Act 1) and 20 (Act 2) of ours; our Act 1 card skip rate is 8 against 15 in 100; in Act 2 we heal at 59 in 100 of rest sites where the humans smith at 54 or more. At human-built fight starts our fighter survives Act 2 elites and hallways as often as the humans (85.3 against 85.7, 96.8 against 94.8; `human-bar/diagnosis.md`). At the Act 2 door the generation-11 player held 8.2 relics and 0.48 potions against 11.6 and 1.68 (not re-measured for 14; the measure lane does). Ranked gaps, each with a test: (1) the deck against the next act's fight pools (fixed and known in Act 1; the human prior reads only the current region's); (2) what a relic does (296 ids); (3) what an event option pays; (4) the potion economy; (5) the dangerous encounter of a pool; then deck role coverage, stronger players' card and relic rates by skill bracket, a scripted enemy's cycle for the judge, elite bonuses. Unused sources with terms: Spire Codex's skill-bracket rates and compendium, STS Tracker, MetaBot, annotated replays, the engine's enemy cycles, the official totals. Not found: public per-act path statistics, Codex bracket numbers, English lists of the Hive's fights, whether the Act 2 boss is shown during Act 1; STS2 expert material is thin. Checker: the memo exists as described, no ids or paths, the fight-start parity and the gen-11 potion figures confirmed in their sources, the HANDOFF line and CRLF confirmed; its one CONTRADICTED (the boss share) was the checker's own error (it counted Act 2 entrants from floor 17, the Act 1 boss fight); recomputed by hand from the exam rows: 496 Act 2 entrants (floor 18 or deeper), 154 died at the Act 2 boss (floor 33) = 31 per 100, 114 at elites = 23.0, 125 in hallways (111 monster, 14 unknown rooms) = 25.2, and the Act 1 elite share of monster-or-elite rooms is 0.55 against 4.84 a run = 10.2 in 100, so the memo's figures for our player stand; the humans' per-100 counts, the gen-11 relic counts (8.2 against 11.6) and the human elite share (29) were not re-derived (they come from the derived rows and earlier notes, not from human.json); the claim that the human prior's forecast reads only the current region's pools was not found in human_prior.py (the forecast lives in the fight net's summary, which lane A confirmed by building H2 from it).

**2. The grade design** (Opus, 00:36Z to 01:00Z; `referee/backlog/act2-build/OBJECTIVE.md`, 2,426 words, design only). The grade: each option is worth its chance of killing the Act 2 boss (the keep test's frontier B2): a continuation that dies 0, one at the Act 2 door `1 - p2`, one at the Act 1 door `(1 - p1) x A2(s)`, won 1, with `p` the judge's P(death) recalibrated per judge; Q = mean over draws x 13.5, in floors-equivalent. A2 = P(kills the Act 2 boss | kills the Act 1 boss, Act 1 state), a small model on the student's row plus H2, the fight net's forecast of the deck against the Hive's fights (Act 2 is always the Hive, addendum 16, confirmed), fitted on human fifths 1 to 3 and our self-play with a source term. Why not floors: `door_return` pays 30.93 for surviving the Act 1 boss whatever the deck (constants confirmed, keys 0 and 1), so relics, upgrades and scaling count only through that boss while an elite's HP is charged in full; on the stored screens the currency change alone leaves the best option unchanged on 93 to 98 in 100 Act 1 screens (so the Act 1 leaf must read the deck) and moves it on 13 to 20 in 100 Act 2 screens with labels as clear as before (the worker's figures; the memo does not state the method, NOT FOUND by the checker). Relabel without the 7-hour re-run: Act 2 screens exact; Act 1 deck kinds on the option's stored row (approximate); Act 1 routes and events not relabelable (the door's deck is not stored), so the teacher keeps a door row per draw from its next block; Act 2 labels from v2b-judged runs only. The checker's counts differ from the memo's by about 2 in 100 (Act 2 rows 3,945 against 4,029; Act 1 16,093 against 16,185; the memo's parts 20,214 against its stated 20,282; a neow-row and capped-run convention, not material). Card head: the frozen human prior plus `alpha x r`, `r` reading the prior's features and H2, `alpha` the largest value at which human log-loss on won runs rises at most 0.001 nats (0.01 on all) on a held-out tenth; the card lambda at play never below 0.5; the pass-or-fail leg becomes a measured size. Test: the keep test unchanged; Look 0 without play (A2's slope on our own Act 2 boss kills, lower bound above 0.5 else stop; human picks explain the new grade; signal to noise by kind); Look 1 pre-registered at the judgement's 500-seed look (about an hour of 16 processes): Act 2 boss reached per 100 up, Act 1 boss kills not down by more than 3; Look 2 the exam's Act 2 columns. Cost: about 2 core-hours of preparation plus one judgement; three Python lanes. Rejected: a re-weighted BEYOND (a constant cannot rank decks), a value model as the whole grade, frontier kills at the door, play-outs on to the Act 2 door (3 to 4 times the CPU). Risks: zero wins is no problem at B2 (our Act 2 boss kill 11 to 13 per 100 runs), noise at K 2 to 4, mixed judges, rows not doors, recklessness (the floor guard and Look 1), the route head in play learned floors and may be where the cautious Act 1 comes from.

**3. The owner's two rules** (01:10Z to 01:30Z), adopted as standing rules. (a) **Information parity**: "the player should have all the info that humans have been using to win at a minimum": at every decision the player's inputs contain what the game shows a human there plus the facts humans routinely derive (the next act's fights, what a relic does, what an event option pays). (b) **Nothing retired on a small label test**: "I just don't want things discarded prematurely that might work at scale": a parked item stays in the stored data and is re-tested whenever the labels or the data change in kind; a label-prediction test on a small store never retires anything; retirement needs a play test at full scale and the owner's word. The owner's question that led to (b): the student's card, starting-bonus (ancient) and rest heads failed leg 2 (drift from winning humans: won +0.0343, +0.0185, +0.0090 against +0.002) while route and shop passed and event and reward passed leg 1 with leg 2 unmeasured, select failed leg 1 for too few decisions (83 of 100); under the floors grade that failure is the expected symptom (the teacher's labels teach caution, winners do the opposite), and the same grade gated the map grid and block G (addendum 23), so "did not move the verdict" meant "the labels do not use it". The orchestrator had wrongly said the boss-relic head failed; corrected to the owner. **Consequence**: once the regraded labels exist, the map grid, block G and the three heads are re-fitted and re-tested together on the new labels before the first exam.

**4. Lanes started** (`referee/backlog/act2-build/`): **A** (Sonnet, 01:00Z, `BRIEF-lane-A.md`): H2 and A2 in `rl/act2_chance.py` with Look 0 (a). **B** (Sonnet, 01:00Z, `BRIEF-lane-B.md`): `frontier_return` beside `door_return` (floors stays the default), the `regrade` sub-command into a new root, door rows per draw from the next teacher block, `run_student.load_shards` refusing two grades; full gate; the first teacher block on it watched. **D** (Sonnet, 01:20Z, `BRIEF-lane-D.md`): information parity as side blocks without growing BASE_SIZE (the screen inventory closed; relic effect tags; deck role counts; event payoffs; the map grid and block G back behind flags; potion odds and the act's boss if exposed), the two teacher stores re-encoded, the human shards' re-encode estimated only. Lane C (the card residual) waits for Look 0. The measure lane (00:36Z) runs on. Starting A and B before the measure lane landed is a departure from "measure first", taken because the memo's death-location reading already confirmed the state-not-play direction, floors stays the default in B, and Look 0 is itself a measurement.

**5. Lane A landed** (01:53Z; fork main `1945e188`, select gate 276 tests, new 0, flakes 0, 40 new tests; `referee/agent/act2/v1/`: `a2.pt` f3cdf09b…, `a2.npz` a5d3da24…, `fit.json`, `report.json`, `report.md`). **Look 0 (a) passes**: the slope of our actual Act 2 boss kill on A2's logit at our Act 1 door, held-out seeds, 0.713 with 95 in 100 interval [0.603, 0.832] (bootstrap over 4,626 runs, 531 kills; the stop rule was a lower bound under 0.5). **2. The lab could not start the student, silently.** The student fit was due by the lab's own rule (`student_hold` None in a shell; `student_due`: 11,028 new labelled screens of 10,000) from about 01:34Z, yet the runner picked the teacher at 01:34Z, 03:01Z and, after a restart, 03:12Z, and logged no reason (`ladder_top` logs a held kind's reason only for the nightly). **A wrong first reading**: the runner process had started 2026-11-11 11:46:54Z, a minute before `57af0514` (the student's ladder hook) reached the lab tree, so the orchestrator restarted it at 03:11:34Z by the `Spireline fix lab` scheduled task (no real-game job, no nightly marker, no hand-started job), killing the running teacher block `20261113T0301-teacher` with its in-flight runs (the finished runs are in the block's parts; about 16 runs of up to 25 CPU-minutes lost); the restarted runner picked the teacher again, so the restart was unnecessary. **The cause**: `student_due` counts the labelled screens by running `rl/run_student.py check` from the runner's own tree (`ROOT`, the fork's main checkout `D:/spireline/fork/sts2`), whose `out/Sts2Emulator.dll` dates from 11-06; since the engine landing of 11-12 (`EngineFaults.cs`) the engine's staleness guard refuses to load it, the check exits 1 ("the check failed"), `student_due` returns "cannot count the labelled screens", and the student is passed over without a line in the log. Reproduced by running the check from the runner's working directory (rc 1 in 0.7 s, the guard's message); from the lab tree it passes (rc 0, 9 s, 20,995 decisions kept). So the loop's student step has not been able to start on its own since 11-12 about 06:45Z. **Fixed in the config** at 03:16Z: `LAB/jobs.json` student `check_cmd` now runs the lab tree's `rl/run_student.py check` (`D:/spireline/fork/wt-lab`, whose `out/` every presort rebuilds) with the runner's Python (`{python}`, `{root}`); atomic edit, backup `jobs.json.2026-11-13-student-check.bak`; verified from the runner's working directory (rc 0; `unready("student")` None). The runner reads `jobs.json` at every pick, so the student is picked when the teacher block `20261113T0312-teacher` ends (about 04:20Z): the student's first pick is expected when the 0312 block ends, read by a one-shot check at 04:33Z, with the 09:37Z check after it. **Follow-ups for the next lab lane** (Python, tests first): the default check command uses the lab tree, not `ROOT`; a kind that is due and held logs its reason once, as the nightly does; the staleness guard's refusal in a check is an alert, not silence.

**3. What today's lanes cost the teacher**, read from the runner log: the judge-switch gate and lanes A, B, D and the measure lane ran beside the teacher; a landing on fork main stops the running teacher block for the presort (01:34Z block cut at 02:05Z after 31 minutes by lane A's landing; the 02:35Z presort by lane D's), lane D's `act2-d` QUIET line (02:04:40Z to 02:58:06Z, taken for its replay under the standing rule) held every lab job for 54 minutes, and the lanes' load paused the runner 02:11Z to 02:15Z (other processes 66 in 100 of the CPU over 120 s). About two hours of the teacher's day. **Rules from this**: a re-encode or a fit never takes a QUIET line, only a job that steps the game at scale does, and every brief says so; landings beside a running teacher are batched where the work allows (the owner's rule); at most two heavy lanes beside the lab at once.

### D59 addendum 3 (2026-11-13T05:01Z): the Act 2 measurement: the hypothesis holds on elites, relics, potions and Act 2 rests, not on scaling cards; the deck's defence and the fight's play share the rest

**Landed** (measure lane, Sonnet, 00:36Z to 04:40Z; `referee/act2-build/2026-11-13-measure/`: `table.md`, `summary.json`, `method.md`, 800 derived run records; fork main `2c2196ca`, a `--records` flag for the seed exam driver, select gate 286, new 0). The 400 human seeds x 2 draws replayed with records, 800 of 800 rows identical to the generation-14 exam's; the humans from the derived rows, paired by seed; 95 in 100 intervals over seeds; ours minus humans.

**Verdicts.** (1) **Elites: confirmed.** Act 1 elites a run 0.55 against 2.01 (-1.46 [-1.58, -1.35]); 10 in 100 of our Act 1 fights against 31; our rooms go to "?" (+1.05) and shops (+0.43) instead. (2) **Relics: confirmed.** At the Act 1 door 4.34 against 6.36; at the Act 2 door 8.59 against 12.33 (-4.08 [-4.66, -3.52]). (3) **Upgrades: Act 2 only.** Act 1 door 3.15 against 3.32 (level); Act 2 door 5.93 against 7.61; in Act 2 ours heals at 1.90 rests and upgrades at 1.24, the humans 0.98 and 1.96. (4) **Scaling cards: not supported.** `scaling_damage`-tagged cards 2.75 against 2.52 at the Act 1 door, 3.70 against 3.63 at the Act 2 door. The deck difference is defence and flow: printed block 40 against 51, 2.2 more attacks, 2.4 fewer skills, fewer draw and energy cards; potions 0.57 against 1.77. (5) **HP hoarding: half true.** At the Act 1 door ours has 63.3 HP against 54.1 and still loses that boss more (kills 66.7 against 86.0 in 100 of those reaching it); at the Act 2 door HP is level (61.1 against 63.4). (6) **Act 2 death: the state and the play.** Damage a turn is level (41 against 42 at elites, 61.6 against 61.7 at bosses); ours loses more HP a fight (33 against 21 at elites, 51 against 35 at bosses) and loses 27 in 100 Act 2 elite fights against 3, 60 in 100 bosses against 11. Holding enemy, start HP, deck counts, relics and potions equal drops the odds ratio of losing from 15.9 to 4.2 [2.3, 8.0]; relics and potions carry the fall, the deck counts none; the remainder is play inside the fight or card quality the counts miss (the fight-level exam of D57 says play is part of it). (7) **Does not fit**: Act 1 upgrades, scaling-tagged cards, damage a turn and Act 2 door HP are level; ours takes energy relics at 49 in 100 of 35 Act 2 ancient offers (the humans' offers under 20, no cell); there is no boss-relic screen in this version, the ancient at the next act's start is that choice; the elite-to-Act-2-boss link is positive in both populations (+0.26 humans, +0.18 ours) but confounded and untestable with 62 of our runs at 2 or more elites; **the teacher's round 1 (305 runs, other seeds) is not better than the student**: the student's exam figures lie inside its intervals on every line. (8) **Not measured**: whether elites cause the better deck (a route-changed run); human hallway fights beyond the 5,000 sample; human removals and state at death; rare relics' classes.

**Reading.** The owner's hypothesis holds where it was about the route and the rewards (elites, relics, potions, Act 2 rests) and about Act 1 caution (HP at the door), and fails where it was about the cards: ours picks scaling damage as the humans do, and loses on defence, card flow and potions, and on play inside Act 2 fights with the same damage a turn. The two memos and this measurement agree on the mechanism to change first: the grade (floors pays nothing for relics, potions or a defence that only matters in Act 2) and the information (relic effects and potions are now side blocks P1 and P4). The teacher ceiling named at 00:30Z is measured: the teacher plays no better than its student, so labels graded by its own play-outs cannot lift the student past it; the new grade's leaf (A2, fitted where the humans win) is the way out of that circle. **A trap for every replay**: `rl/data/card_tags.json`, the cache `rl/card_tags.py` writes, changes the combat net's inputs (`rl/combat_tokens.py` CARD_TAGS_PATH): with it present a first replay diverged from the exam; absent in fork main and the lab tree now, but a fighter whose inputs depend on an optional cache file is a byte-identity hazard; follow-up for the next fork lane: the net never reads that cache, or the cache is part of the pinned inputs. Checker: every claim confirmed: the 800 replayed rows match the exam's on floor, end and win (0 differ, keyed by seed and draw), every headline figure found in table.md or summary.json (the damage-a-turn and Act 2 loss figures in the reading's prose, no separate cell), no human seed or run id in the outputs, every cell at 20 or more (smallest 22), the commit and its gate, the card_tags.json trap documented and the file absent from both trees, the HANDOFF line and 0 bare LF, no leftover process or lock; the source-checkout scan was not run (off limits for the checker).

**Decisions.** (1) The grade switch proceeds as designed (addendum 1): when lane B lands, Look 0 (b) to (d) on the regraded store with the parity blocks P1 and P4 available to the fit; the first candidate on the new grade is judged after the lab's own generation-15 judgement on floors (one candidate at a time), its Look 1 pre-registered as the memo says. (2) The card head's change (lane C) stays as designed, with the measurement's note that the card difference is defence and flow, not scaling; `alpha` is the number to read. (3) Elites as a cause stay unmeasured until a route-changed run is cheap; the new grade's first look (Act 2 boss reached) is the test that matters. (4) Removable, the owner's call: worktrees `wt-act2-a`, `wt-act2-d`, the measure lane's worktree, `D:/spireline/referee/act2-build/2026-11-13-measure/replay/`.

### D59 addendum 4 (2026-11-13T06:39Z): the loop's student step ran on its own for the first time and failed on a wrong prior; the three run-loop fixes landed; the runner restarted on them

**The first autonomous student pick** (04:57:40Z, after the 03:16Z stopgap let the screen count pass): `20261113T0457-student` and `20261113T045748-student` each failed in 8 s (rc 1) with `run_student.ShardError: the log-probabilities the teacher recorded differ from this prior's by up to 4.7342`; after two failures the lab wrote `LAB/alerts/student.txt` and stopped picking the student, and the teacher ran on. **Cause**: the lab's job passes `--prior` = the incumbent's start prior, which for generation 14 is its own student file (`student_base`: "its student's file when it has one, else its human prior's"), but the teacher records the human prior's log-probabilities (`rl/run_teacher.py` line 876: the pull `lam * logp` uses pi_h), and `attach_prior` refuses a store whose recorded `logp` is not the given prior's. Round 0's incumbent (generation 13) had no student file, so the prior was the human prior and the check passed; round 1 is the first round with a student incumbent, and the job never passed the second file although `run_fit` takes `teacher_prior_path` for exactly this. Verified on the real store at 05:15Z: `run_student.npz` alone fails (4.7342); `human_prior.npz` alone passes (max difference 5e-06, 9,000 decisions checked); `prior=run_student.npz` with `teacher_prior=human_prior.npz` passes the same.

**Lane E landed** (Sonnet, 05:20Z to 06:18Z; fork main `3d8f57b5`; full gate 5,886 tests, new 0, 1 flake that passes alone; `docs/lab/RUN_LOOP.md` updated; checker: every claim confirmed but a one-minute timestamp and two test names). (2) `student_check_cmd` runs `rl/run_student.py` from the job's own tree (`{tree}`, set from the runner's `--tree`), `check_cmd` still an override: addendum 2's stale-library hold cannot recur. (3) `ladder_top` logs `<kind>: due but held (<reason>)` once per distinct reason for the student and the teacher, and again when a hold ends and returns. `LAB/jobs.json` edited 06:17Z in one atomic write (backup `jobs.json.2026-11-13-lab-fixes.bak`): the student command gains `--teacher-prior {student_teacher_prior}`; the stopgap `check_cmd` and its note removed.

**Runner restart** at 06:36:45Z (the `Spireline fix lab` task stopped and started during the presort that followed the 04:57Z teacher block's end at 06:36:03Z, so no teacher run was lost; no real-game job, no nightly marker): the running runner held the old `jobs.py`, which would have left the token unfilled and counted screens from its own stale checkout. `LAB/alerts/student.txt` removed at the same moment (the lab's own instruction). **Reading.** Two faults, both of the kind "the first time this path runs on its own": the stale-library check (addendum 2) and the second-round prior. Neither was visible in the log; both are now (fix 3). The loop's student step has still never completed on its own; the first judgement it produces is the baseline for the new grade's candidate (addendum 3).
